‘‘(b) L IMITATIONS ON CLAIMS.—Notwithstanding  5

‘‘(1) is filed not later than 150 days after the  8

‘‘(B) in the case of any other claim, the  15

‘‘(2) in the case of an authorization for which  17

which the opportunity for public comment occurs  19
after the date of enactment of the Bipartisan Amer -20
ican Affordability and Jobs Act of 2026, the claim  21

SEC. 1133. PERMITTING IMPROVEMENT FUND.  9
(a) E STABLISHMENT.—There is established in the  10
Treasury of the United States a fund, to be known as the  11
‘‘Permitting Improvement Fund’’ (referred to in this sec -12
tion as the ‘‘Fund’’). 13
(b) P URPOSE.—The purpose of the Fund is to im -14
prove the performance of key permitting agencies with re -15
spect to timely environmental reviews and authorizations  16
required under the National Environmental Policy Act of  17
1969 (42 U.S.C. 4321 et seq.). 18
(c) T RANSFER AUTHORITY.—Amounts in the Fund  19
shall be available to the Chairman to transfer to a key  20
permitting agency for 1 or more uses described in sub -21
section (d). 22
(d) U SE OF AMOUNTS.—Amounts transferred under  23
subsection (c) shall be used by a key permitting agency—  24
===== PAGE 108 =====
108 
KAT26764 MSK  S.L.C. 
(1) to support interagency detailee and rotation  1
opportunities; 2
(2) to facilitate professional development;  3
(3) to enhance support for agency project man -4
agers; 5
(4) to support standardizing processes among  6
regional and field offices;  7
(5) to improve opportunities for public engage -8
ment; or 9
(6) for any other use, as determined by the  10
Chairman, consistent with the purpose of the Fund.  11
(e) EFFECT ON DECISIONMAKING.—The use of funds  12
under subsection (d) by a key permitting agency shall not  13
impact the impartial decisionmaking of the key permitting  14
agency with respect to environmental reviews or authoriza -15
tions, either procedurally or substantively. 16
(f) A UTHORIZATION OF APPROPRIATION.—There is  17
authorized to be appropriated to the Fund $20,000,000  18
for each of fiscal years 2027 through 2033. 19

(1) C OVERED APPLICATION .—The term ‘‘cov -19
ered application’’ means a complete application for  20

SEC. 2107. RATEPAYER PROTECTION. 3
(a) FERC P RICING POLICY FOR TRANSMISSION 4
SERVICES PROVIDED BY PUBLIC UTILITIES AND TRANS-5
MITTING UTILITIES.—  6
(1) FINDINGS.—Congress finds that—  7
(A) it is the policy of the United States  8
that computational load owners and computa -9
tional load operators bear the full cost of any  10
electric service that they require and the full  11
cost of any infrastructure built to serve them,  12
regardless of whether they take the electric  13
service that they request, so that such costs are  14
not borne by other customers, including resi -15
dential customers; 16
(B) it is not just and reasonable within the  17
meaning of sections 205 and 206 of the Federal  18
Power Act (16 U.S.C. 824d, 824e) for any pub -19
lic utility or transmitting utility to maintain  20
rates, terms, or conditions of service that, by  21
their terms, charge less than the embedded  22
costs and incremental expansion costs to eligible  23
computational load customers seeking trans -24
===== PAGE 282 =====
282 
KAT26764 MSK  S.L.C. 
mission service on behalf of computational load  1
owners and computational load operators;  2
(C) transmission costs incurred in response  3
to transmission service from eligible computa -4
tional load customers should be fully assigned  5
to such customers so as to minimize or avoid  6
the cost burden on other customers; and  7
(D) computational load owners and com -8
putational load operators should incur the full  9
cost of the transmission service they require.  10

SEC. 2109. GRID-ENHANCING ADVANCED TRANSMISSION  3
TECHNOLOGIES. 4
Part II of the Federal Power Act (16 U.S.C. 824 et  5
seq.) (as amended by section 2108(d)) is amended by add -6
ing at the end the following: 7
‘‘SEC. 232. USE OF GRID-ENHANCING ADVANCED TRANS -8
MISSION TECHNOLOGIES. 9
‘‘(a) DEFINITIONS.—In this section:  10
‘‘(1) BEST-AVAILABLE CLASS OF TRANSMISSION  11
CONDUCTOR.—The term ‘best-available class of  12
transmission conductor’ means a class of high-per -13
formance transmission conductors that, as deter -14
mined by the Commission—  15
‘‘(A) provides the greatest feasible and  16
commercially available energy-carrying capacity  17
at a given voltage level;  18
‘‘(B) provides the highest feasible and  19
commercially available electrical efficiency at  20
that voltage level;  21
‘‘(C) provides the greatest feasible and  22
commercially available level of mechanical per -23
formance, taking into account relevant factors,  24
===== PAGE 319 =====
319 
KAT26764 MSK  S.L.C. 
including reliability, safety, and affordability;  1
and 2
‘‘(D) provides an economically reasonable  3
alternative to a transmission conductor that is  4
not a high-performance transmission conductor.  5
‘‘(2) C OMMISSION.—The term ‘Commission’  6
means the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission.  7
‘‘(3) C OVERED PROJECT .—The term ‘covered  8
project’ means the construction of a new, or the  9
modification, upgrade, replacement, or  10
reconductoring of an existing, transmission facility  11
subject to the jurisdiction of the Commission under  12
section 201(b). 13
‘‘(4) G RID-ENHANCING ADVANCED TRANS -14
MISSION TECHNOLOGY .—The term ‘grid-enhancing  15
advanced transmission technology’ means—  16
‘‘(A) dynamic line rating;  17
‘‘(B) advanced power flow control;  18
‘‘(C) transmission topology optimization;  19
and 20
‘‘(D) high-performance transmission con -21
ductors. 22
‘‘(b) U SE OF GRID-ENHANCING ADVANCED TRANS-23
MISSION TECHNOLOGIES.—In exercising its authority  24
===== PAGE 320 =====
320 
KAT26764 MSK  S.L.C. 
under sections 205 and 206, the Commission shall require  1
that public utilities—  2
‘‘(1) evaluate their transmission systems to as -3
sess all technically achievable opportunities to deploy  4
grid-enhancing advanced transmission technologies,  5
including the costs and benefits of such deployment;  6
and 7
‘‘(2) when carrying out covered projects, use  8
grid-enhancing advanced transmission technologies,  9
including transmission conductors in the best-avail -10
able class of transmission conductors, whenever the  11
benefits of doing so would exceed the costs.  12
‘‘(c) P RESUMPTION OF PRUDENCE FOR GRID-EN-13
HANCING ADVANCED TRANSMISSION TECHNOLOGIES.—In  14
any filing seeking to recover the cost of a grid-enhancing  15
advanced transmission technology for a covered project,  16
and in any proceeding to determine whether that cost may  17
be recovered through rates, the Commission shall presume  18
that—  19
‘‘(1) the use of a grid-enhancing advanced  20
transmission technology is a prudent practice;  21
‘‘(2) the costs associated with the use of a grid-  22
enhancing advanced transmission technology are just  23
and reasonable; and  24
===== PAGE 321 =====
321 
KAT26764 MSK  S.L.C. 
‘‘(3) the costs associated with the use of a grid-  1
enhancing advanced transmission technology provide  2
an economically reasonable alternative to the use of  3
transmission technologies that are not grid-enhanc -4
ing advanced transmission technologies.  5
‘‘(d) APPLICABILITY.—The requirements of this sec -6
tion apply only to public utilities and only with respect  7
to covered projects. 8
‘‘(e) RULEMAKING.—  9
‘‘(1) IN GENERAL .—Not later than 1 year after  10
the date of enactment of this section, the Commis -11
sion shall issue an order or rule to implement this  12
section. 13

‘‘(A) require that each public utility con -16
ducts, on Commission-established timelines, a  17

‘‘(i) to determine when the benefits  20

===== PAGE 322 =====

that the applicable benefit-cost ratio is  1

‘‘(ii) that evaluates whether the costs  3

plicable covered projects; and  6
‘‘(iii) that considers planning and con -7

‘‘(B) require that, for any covered project  11

the Commission; 19
‘‘(C) require that decisions on whether to  20

(ii) the reliability of the data;  16
(iii) whether estimates and projections  17

the conclusions reached.  21

taking into consideration the timeline of the applica -25

KAT26764 MSK  S.L.C. 
ble State, local, or Tribal government for consider -1

(a) DEFINITIONS.—In this section:  8
